But no, I wouldn't say I get sudden fear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of intense fear or anxiety that lasted for a short time, even if it didn't seem to come on suddenly?\n\nPatient: No, I... I haven't really had anything like that. It's not a short thing, it's just... well, a constant sort of worry, I suppose. Not intense periods.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about social situations or being judged by others?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I... I don't think about that much. I'm not really concerned with what people think of me, I suppose. It's just... other things.\n\nDoctor: Is your worry usually about one particular thing or are you worrying about a wide range of things?\n\nPatient: Well... it's a bit of everything, really. I suppose my mind just sort of... roams. I'll be thinking about the schedule for the local art exhibition, and then I'm worrying about my daughter's travel plans, and then... well, then I'm wondering if the gutters on the house need fixing. Just... a wide range of things. And these small things, they just start to feel like they could... unravel, I guess. Into something much bigger.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. I get a bit... wound up, sometimes. Even just sitting in my own living room. I'll find myself tapping my fingers if I'm on the phone, or... getting up to straighten things around the room. Just... sitting quietly makes me feel a bit jumpy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tense in your muscles?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yes. Mostly by the evening, I notice it. My shoulders and my neck get quite tight. I guess I spend the day sort of... braced, without realizing it. I'll just be sitting there reading, or trying to sort out activities for the garden center, and I'll realize I've been clenching my jaw. So... yes, they do get a bit tight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had what you would describe as an anxiety attack?\n\nPatient: An attack? Well... no, I don't think so. I just... I get a bit worked up, like I said. But nothing I would really call an attack, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to concentrate on your daily tasks due to your worries?\n\nPatient: Concentrate? I... no, I don't think so. I manage to get through my day alright. I can still sit and read, or... do what needs doing. It doesn't really get in the way of that, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you tend to avoid certain situations or activities because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Avoid things? No... I wouldn't say that. I still get out and do my errands, or... whatever needs doing. I just carry on with things, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worry is disproportionate to the actual problems you're facing?\n\nPatient: Disproportionate? I... well, I suppose some people might think so. But to me, they're just things I need to think about. I don't know if I'd use that word.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I suppose my mind just sort of... roams. Into something much bigger.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. I get a bit... wound up, sometimes. Even just sitting in my own living room. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF41.1, F41.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello. Um... well, I suppose it's just that I can't seem to turn off my worrying. Once it starts, I just... I can't stop it. Even when I tell myself that I've already made the lists, checked all the dates, and done everything I reasonably can... it just keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose they do. Um... my mind just sort of roams. I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule, and then... my daughter's travel plans, or whether the house gutters need fixing. Just... small things, really. But they start to feel as if they could... unravel into something much bigger, even when I try to focus on something else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors in response to these worries?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't do anything like that. I just... well, I just sit there and think about it too much, I suppose. But there aren't any... rituals, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever experience sudden, intense episodes of fear or discomfort that reach a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. It doesn't really happen suddenly like that. It's just... always sort of there in the background. But no, I wouldn't say I get sudden fear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of intense fear or anxiety that lasted for a short time, even if it didn't seem to come on suddenly?\n\nPatient: No, I... I haven't really had anything like that. It's not a short thing, it's just... well, a constant sort of worry, I suppose. Not intense periods.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about social situations or being judged by others?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I... I don't think about that much. I'm not really concerned with what people think of me, I suppose. It's just... other things.\n\nDoctor: Is your worry usually about one particular thing or are you worrying about a wide range of things?\n\nPatient: Well... it's a bit of everything, really. I suppose my mind just sort of... roams. I'll be thinking about the schedule for the local art exhibition, and then I'm worrying about my daughter's travel plans, and then... well, then I'm wondering if the gutters on the house need fixing. Just... a wide range of things. And these small things, they just start to feel like they could... unravel, I guess. Into something much bigger.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. I get a bit... wound up, sometimes. Even just sitting in my own living room. I'll find myself tapping my fingers if I'm on the phone, or... getting up to straighten things around the room. Just... sitting quietly makes me feel a bit jumpy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tense in your muscles?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yes. Mostly by the evening, I notice it. My shoulders and my neck get quite tight. I guess I spend the day sort of... braced, without realizing it. I'll just be sitting there reading, or trying to sort out activities for the garden center, and I'll realize I've been clenching my jaw. So... yes, they do get a bit tight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had what you would describe as an anxiety attack?\n\nPatient: An attack? Well... no, I don't think so. I just... I get a bit worked up, like I said. But nothing I would really call an attack, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to concentrate on your daily tasks due to your worries?\n\nPatient: Concentrate? I... no, I don't think so. I manage to get through my day alright. I can still sit and read, or... do what needs doing. It doesn't really get in the way of that, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you tend to avoid certain situations or activities because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Avoid things? No... I wouldn't say that. I still get out and do my errands, or... whatever needs doing. I just carry on with things, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worry is disproportionate to the actual problems you're facing?\n\nPatient: Disproportionate? I... well, I suppose some people might think so. But to me, they're just things I need to think about. I don't know if I'd use that word.\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying ever interfered with your ability to enjoy leisure activities?\n\nPatient: Interfered? Um... no, I wouldn't say so. I still have my hobbies. I read, or... do a bit of gardening. I still enjoy them well enough. It doesn't really get in the way of that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying affects your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: My relationships? Um... no, not really. I mean, I still see my family, talk to my friends... it's all fine. I just... keep things to myself, mostly. So, no, I don't think it affects them.\n\nDoctor: Does your worry ever make it hard for you to fall asleep or stay asleep at night?\n\nPatient: My sleep? Um... no, I usually sleep fine. I mean, sometimes I wake up in the middle of the night, but... I think that's just getting older. I don't really lie awake thinking about things. So, my sleep is alright.\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying ever caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Distress? I... I don't know about distress. I mean, I still do my garden, and... I still go to my book club. It's just... I suppose I don't always finish the books like I used to. And I told the ladies I couldn't help plan the spring exhibition this year. It just... felt like a bit too much to manage right now. But I wouldn't call it distress.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Even when I tell myself that I've already made the lists, checked all the dates, and done everything I reasonably can... it just keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose they do. Um... my mind just sort of roams. I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule, and then... my daughter's travel plans, or whether the house gutters need fixing. Just... small things, really. But they start to feel as if they could... unravel into something much bigger, even when I try to focus on something else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors in response to these worries?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't do anything like that. I just... well, I just sit there and think about it too much, I suppose. But there aren't any... rituals, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever experience sudden, intense episodes of fear or discomfort that reach a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. It doesn't really happen suddenly like that. It's just... always sort of there in the background. I haven't really had anything like that. It's not a short thing, it's just... well, a constant sort of worry, I suppose. I suppose my mind just sort of... roams. Just... a wide range of things. And these small things, they just start to feel like they could... unravel, I guess. Even when I tell myself that I've already made the lists, checked all the dates, and done everything I reasonably can... it just keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose they do. I don't do anything like that. I just... well, I just sit there and think about it too much, I suppose. I haven't really had anything like that. It's not a short thing, it's just... well, a constant sort of worry, I suppose. I'm not really concerned with what people think of me, I suppose. It's just... other things.\n\nDoctor: Is your worry usually about one particular thing or are you worrying about a wide range of things?\n\nPatient: Well... it's a bit of everything, really. I suppose my mind just sort of... roams.
